About US
Fabtech Technologies Limited (FTL), the flagship of the
Fabtech Group, brings three decades of engineering excel-
lence in delivering start-to-finish turnkey solutions encom-
passing cleanroom infrastructure, HVAC systems, modular
panels, process utilities, mechanical-electrical-plumbing
(MEP) services, pharmaceutical machinery, and complete
turnkey execution for pharmaceutical, biotech, and health-
care facilities worldwide. With deep capabilities across
Process, Air & Water, we integrate design, engineering, man-
ufacturing, and validation to build GMP-compliant,
energy-efficient, and contamination-controlled environ-
ments across countries.
Our asset-light, integrated model combines in-house manu-
facturing, advanced project execution, and minimal
third-party dependency to ensure speed, quality, and
consistency. Since our inception, we have developed signifi-
cant expertise in executing highly specialized pharmaceuti-
cal manufacturing plants, biotechnology facilities, vaccine
production units, sterile injectable facilities, oral solid dosage
plants, laboratories, and healthcare infrastructure projects.
Our integrated business model enables single-window
project execution, ensuring efficiency, quality assurance,
cost optimization, and timely delivery.
What Sets Us Apart
ISO-Certified Operation Start-To-End Turnkey Engineering Solutions Provider
• In-House Design & Manufacturing Excellence
Trusted By Leading Pharmaceutical & Biotech Companies
3 Decades of Fabtech Group's Legacy

Global Presence
Over the years, Fabtech has expanded its global footprint and today operates across more than 63 countries, serving clients
throughout the Middle East, Africa, and other international markets.
Deriving 78% of our revenue from MENA, GCC, and ECO Zone markets, we continue to expand through FTS Cleanrooms
Systems LLC and strategic joint ventures, strengthening our position as a preferred partner for critical facility infrastructure.
The Company derives substantial revenues from diverse geographical markets including, Saudi Arabia, UAE, Kenya, Algeria,
Egypt, and other international regions, reflecting its growing global acceptance and execution capabilities.

Process Flow

of Cleanroom Infrastructure Data Sheet with the Finalisation of Quotes Placing of Orders

Engineering Precision, Delivered: The Fabtech Execution Blueprint
At Fabtech, project execution is the crucible in which our engineering philosophy is tested and proven. It is a meticulously calibrated, end-to-end
value chain — spanning opportunity identification to final commissioning — engineered to deliver precision, reliability, and consistency on every
project we undertake. This disciplined framework is the foundation of the trust our clients place in us, and the platform on which we intend to scale
as a listed enterprise.
1. Lead Generation
Our growth engine is powered by a proactive market outreach strategy, enabling us to identify and capture opportunities across diverse industry
verticals and geographies, building a robust and sustainable order pipeline.
2. Feasibility Study
Every opportunity is subjected to a rigorous feasibility assessment, ensuring technical viability, commercial soundness, and strategic alignment
before it is inducted into our execution pipeline — a critical filter that safeguards project quality from the outset.
3. Passing of Enquiry to the Pre-Designing Team
Validated enquiries are seamlessly transitioned to our specialised pre-design team, ensuring continuity of information and a smooth handover
that lays the foundation for accurate and efficient engineering.
4. Design and Engineering
This is where Fabtech's core engineering strength comes to the fore. Our multidisciplinary design team translates client requirements into precise,
optimised, and technically sound engineering solutions, leveraging domain expertise accumulated over years of complex project delivery.
5. Order Finalisation
Design outputs are converted into a definitive, client-approved project scope and order, establishing clear technical and commercial parameters
that guide every subsequent stage of execution.
6. In-House Manufacturing of Cleanroom Infrastructure and Critical Equipment
A key differentiator in Fabtech's execution model is our in-house manufacturing capability for cleanroom infrastructure and critical equipment. This
vertical integration allows us to exercise direct control over quality, design fidelity, and delivery timelines for mission-critical components, reducing
dependency on external sourcing and reinforcing the reliability of our project outcomes.
7. Sharing of Technical Data Sheet with the Procurernent Tearn
For components sourced externally, comprehensive technical data sheets are handed over to our procurement function, ensuring that sourcing
decisions are firmly anchored in engineering specifications and quality benchmarks.
8. Negotiation and Finalisation of Quotes Received from Vendors
Our procurement team leverages an established and reliable vendor ecosystem to negotiate competitive commercial terms, ensuring optimal
cost efficiency without compromising on quality or delivery timelines.
9. Placing of Orders with the Vendors
Orders are placed with a curated network of qualified vendors, reflecting Fabtech's long-standing relationships and rigorous vendor evaluation
standards.
IO. Quality Check / Factory Acceptance Test (FAT)
Every equipment and material — whether manufactured in-house or sourced externally — undergoes stringent quality checks and Factory Accep-
tance Testing, reinforcing our unwavering commitment to delivering products that meet the highest standards of safety, performance, and
durability.
11. Packaging and Shipping
Equipment is packaged and shipped with meticulous attention to handling and logistics protocols, ensuring assets reach project sites in optimal
condition, on schedule.
12. Installation of Equipment and Materials through Contractors
On-site installation is executed through a trusted network of experienced contractors, working under close supervision to ensure adherence to
design specifications and project timelines.
13. Commissioning and Completion of the Project
The process culminates in comprehensive commissioning, where systems are tested, validated, and handed over — marking the successful
delivery of a project that meets client expectations and reinforces Fabtech's reputation for execution excellence.

Achievements
01. FY 2025—26: A Year of Scale, Strength and Strategic Growth
FY 2025—26 marked a defining year for Fabtech Technologies Limited,
strengthening its public market presence and growth trajectory. On a consolidated
basis, the Company reported Revenue from Operations of ü410.77 crore and Net
Profit after Tax of t38.36 crore. Despite global supply chain disruptions and a volatile
macroeconomic environment, Fabtech maintained healthy profitability through
resilient execution and disciplined operational management.
02. IPO in 2025: A New Chapter of Growth
The successful IPO in 2025 was a landmark milestone in Fabtech's journey.
It strengthened the Company's market presence, governance framework and
stakeholder confidence, while opening a new chapter for accelerated growth
across pharmaceutical, biopharmaceutical and healthcare infrastructure markets.
03. t900+ Cr Order Book: Strong Execution Visibility
As of 31st March 2026, Fabtech's consolidated order book stood at over t900 crore,
reflecting strong business momentum and healthy execution visibility. This
achievement came despite global volatility, freight disruptions and input cost
pressures, demonstrating the resilience of Fabtech's execution model and the
strength of its market position.
04. Strategic Project Wins in Global Markets
During the year, Fabtech secured several high-impact international orders across
priority sectors and geographies. Two key wins reflect the Company's growing
strength in regulated and complex infrastructure.
05. Animal Vaccine Project, Saudi Arabia
Fabtech secured an international order of approximately t65.52 crore for cleanroom
infrastructure, HVAC, MEP and utilities for an Animal Vaccine Project in Dammam 3rd
Industrial City, Saudi Arabia. The project reinforces Fabtech's growing role in the
Kingdom's pharmaceutical and animal health infrastructure sector.
06. Oral Solid Dosage Facility, West Africa
Fabtech also secured an international order of approximately Ü63.6 crore for the
supply, installation and commissioning of infrastructure for an Oral Solid Dosage
facility in West Africa. This win reflects Fabtech's continued contribution to
strengthening local pharmaceutical manufacturing capability across Africa
Page IO

[page 12]
Fabtech
Technologies Limited
Annual Report 2025-26 | fabtechnologies.com Life Engineering
07. Next-Generation Veterinary Facility, North Africa
Fabtech also secured an international order of approximately ü49-52 crore for the
development of an EU-GMP-compliant veterinary formulations facility in North Africa.
This project will strengthen regional pharmaceutical manufacturing capabilities
while enabling exports to the EU and neighbouring African markets.
08. Fabtech Lifecare: Strengthening Presence in Saudi Arabia
Fabtech further reinforced its commitment to Saudi Arabia through Fabtech Lifecare,
its established local entity in the Kingdom. Strategically located in Al Malaz, Riyadh,
Fabtech Lifecare enables faster project support, stronger coordination with clients
and regulatory bodies, and region-specific execution backed by Fabtech's global
expertise across 63+ countries. The entity is fully registered in Saudi Arabia, reflecting
Fabtech's compliance and long-term commitment to the region.
09. KP Group Collaboration: Advancing Green Infrastructure
During the year, Fabtech Group also entered into a strategic collaboration with KP
Group to build next-generation green infrastructure across the pharma, biotech
and healthcare sectors. Under this alliance, KP Group will provide and integrate
renewable energy solutions including solar, wind, hybrid, Battery Energy Storage
Systems and green hydrogen-based power solutions for Fabtech Group's life sciences
and healthcare projects. The collaboration strengthens Fabtech's commitment to
sustainable, Iow-carbon and future-ready infrastructure across India, the Middle East
and Africa.
10. Moving Ahead
With a successful IPO, a strong t900+ crore order book, strategic global project wins,
an established Saudi presence and a sustainability-focused alliance with KP Group,
Fabtech Technologies Limited enters its next phase with confidence, focused on
building reliable, future-ready healthcare and pharmaceutical manufacturing
capability where it is needed most.

Chairperson
A YEAR OF QUIET COMPOUNDING
FY 2025—26 was not a year of loud declarations. It was a year of quiet compounding — of structural moves made with deliberate
intent.
We completed our IPO and listed on both NSE and BSE, a milestone that marks not an arrival, but an acceleration. We expanded
our consolidated revenues to lakhs. We deepened our presence across the GCC, East Africa, and emerging markets while
simultaneously building the institutional scaffolding that sustainable global growth demands. We did this while maintaining
earnings quality, strengthening our balance sheet, and distributing our first dividend as a listed company.
Numbers matter. But what the numbers reflect matters more — and what they reflect here is organisational maturity meeting
market opportunity.
THE STRUCTURAL MOMENT
The life sciences sector is in the middle of a structural renaissance. Biologics, mRNA platforms, specialty and sterile manufacturing,
precision medicine, decentralised production — these are not future hypotheticals. They are present realities reshaping capital alloca-
tion decisions of pharmaceutical companies globally. Regulatory bodies are demanding more sophisticated infrastructure. Health-
care systems are demanding more resilient supply chains. Emerging economies are demanding domestic manufacturing capability.
Page 13

[page 15]
Fabtech
Technologies Limited
Annual Report 2025-26 | fabtechnologies.com Life Engineering
GEOGRAPHY AS THESIS, NOT HEDGE
Our international revenues are not a hedge. They are a thesis. A thesis that the next wave of pharmaceutical infrastructure
investment will be led not by the mature markets of Europe and North America, but by the ambitious, growing, infrastruc-
ture-hungry economies of the Middle East, Africa, and Asia. We have been executing in these markets for years — not as an
experiment, but as a strategic conviction.
Kenya, Saudi Arabia, the I-JAE, Egypt, Algeria — these are not exotic outposts on our revenue map. They are deliberate positions in
tomorrow's most consequential growth corridors. The GCC alone is deploying hundreds of billions in economic diversification.
Healthcare localisation is central to every Vision 2030-class national agenda in our operating regions. We are not chasing this
wave. We helped shape it.
SCALE WITH DISCIPLINE
Scale without depth is fragile. Growth without governance is dangerous.
This is perhaps the most important thing I want to communicate to our shareholders. As we grow, our commitment to institution-
al discipline only intensifies. Working capital management, project execution rigour, talent development, technology adoption,
and ethical governance — these are not constraints on our ambition. They are the architecture of it.
"It's not supposed to be easy. Anyone who finds it easy is stupid"
— Charlie Munger
Building a globally respected engineering company in a demanding, regulated, technically complex industry is not easy. We are
not looking for easy. We are building for enduring.
ON OUR PEOPLE
On our people — I will be brief, because brevity here is the only form of honesty.
What we have delivered could not have been delivered by strategy alone. Strategy is paper. Execution is people. The Fabtech
team — across Mumbai, across our subsidiaries, across project sites on four continents — has demonstrated a quality that no
balance sheet captures: they show up, they solve, they deliver. That is the culture. Protect it. Invest in it. Nothing else compounds
quite like it.
WHAT LIES AHEAD
"The most dangerous moment comes with victory."
— Napoleon Bonaparte
We are mindful of this. The risks ahead are real — global macroeconomic volatility, competitive intensification, regulatory
complexity, execution at scale. We do not take our position for granted. We earned it, and we intend to keep earning it.
What gives me genuine confidence — not the ceremonial kind that chairmen are expected to project, but the grounded,
evidence-based kind — is the intersection of where the world is going and what Fabtech is built to do. Rarely does strategic
positioning align this cleanly with macro tailwinds. When it does, the obligation is to execute without distraction.
We began this year as a company newly listed. We will close the next chapter as a company firmly established.
The infrastructure of life sciences is being rebuilt globally. Fabtech will be at the centre of that rebuilding.
WITH GRATITUDE
None of this journey has been solitary.
To our promoter family — whose vision conceived Fabtech, whose conviction carried it through the years before the world was
watching, and whose values remain our moral compass — this milestone belongs first to you. The listing is simply the world
catching up to what you built.
To my colleagues on the Board, our management team, and every Fabtech employee — for the wisdom, the governance, and
the relentless execution that turns strategy into delivered reality, project after project, continent after continent.
To our customers, bankers, vendors and partners — for trust that is anything but transactional.
And to our shareholders — welcome to a story that is only getting more interesting. We intend to be worthy of your belief, one
quarter at a time.
Building Infrastructure. Enabling Science. Engineering Life.

Employee Engagement
Programmes
At Fabtech Technologies Limited, our people remain at the heart of everything we do. Throughout the year, we continued to
create meaningful employee engagement initiatives that brought our Life Engineers together to celebrate achievements,
embrace diverse traditions, prioritise well-being, contribute to society, and strengthen bonds across teams. Through celebra-
tions, wellness initiatives, learning opportunities, and team-building activities, we fostered a culture of belonging, collabora-
tion, and shared purpose. These moments enriched everyday work life and reflected Fabtech's commitment to being a
vibrant, inclusive, and people-centric organisation.
Birthday Celebrations
Celebrating birthdays is our way of acknowledging every Life Engineer as an important part of the Fabtech family. These small
yet meaningful celebrations helped create moments of joy, appreciation, and togetherness, strengthening interpersonal
connections and making employees feel valued beyond their professional contributions.
Festival Celebrations
Fabtech celebrated festivals such as Diwali, Christmas, Eid, and other cultural occasions with enthusiasm and unity. These
celebrations reflected the diversity within our teams and encouraged employees to come together in the spirit of respect,
inclusion, and shared happiness, reinforcing our culture of harmony and belonging.
Indoor Game Tournament
To encourage team bonding and workplace recreation, Fabtech organised indoor game tournaments during the year. These
activities provided employees with an opportunity to unwind, engage in friendly competition, and build stronger relationships
across departments, supporting a healthy and energetic work environment.
Blood Donation Camp
As part of our commitment to social responsibility, Fabtech organised a blood donation camp, encouraging Life Engineers to
contribute towards a meaningful cause. The initiative reflected the compassion and responsibility of our people, demonstrat-
ing that our commitment to saving lives extends beyond the solutions we build.
Technical Training and Skill Development
Fabtech continued to invest in technical training programmes aimed at enhancing the skills, knowledge, and capabilities of
our Life Engineers. These learning initiatives helped employees stay aligned with evolving industry standards, strengthen
technical excellence, and contribute more effectively to the organisation's growth and project execution capabilities.
Women's Day Celebration
On International Women's Day, Fabtech celebrated the contribution, strength, and achievements of women across the organ-
isation. The celebration served as a reminder of our continued commitment to building an inclusive workplace where every
individual is respected, encouraged, and empowered to grow.

CSR Projects Undertaken during FY 2025-26
Corporate Social Responsibility: Engineering Impact Beyond Business
At Fabtech, our responsibility extends beyond the projects we build for clients to the communities we serve. In FY 2025-26, we
supported four CSR initiatives focused on water conservation, education, and healthcare.
1. Water Harvesting Structure — Project Karandi
In partnership with NAAM Foundation, Fabtech supported the desilting of two percolation tanks in Karandi village, Pune. This
added 5.9 crore litres of water storage capacity in a drought-prone area. The project benefits over 1,000 villagers by improving
groundwater levels, supporting farming, and securing long-term water access for the community.
2. Sanskar Public School — Digital Smart Boards
Fabtech partnered with Sanskar Siksha Samiti to install six digital smart boards in classrooms. This helps teachers deliver more
engaging lessons and gives students early exposure to digital learning tools, improving overall learning outcomes.
3. CanAhaar — Nutritional Support for Cancer Patients
Working with Sanjeevani — Life Beyond Cancer, Fabtech provided nutritional support to 40 underprivileged patients undergoing
chemotherapy. Each patient received nutrition kits for their entire treatment cycle, helping them stay strong through a difficult
period.
4. School Kits for Rural Children
In partnership with Shivprabha Charitable Trust, Fabtech sponsored 2,000 school kits for underprivileged children across rural
Maharashtra, including Thane, Yavatmal, Washim, Chandrapur, Gadchiroli, and Ratnagiri. These kits help remove barriers to
regular school attendance and support children's education.

PERFORMANCE AND STA TE OF COMPANY'S AFFAIRS:
During the year under review, the Company expanded its global footprint and strengthened its presence across more
than 62 countries spanning the Middle East, Africa, Asia, Europe, Latin America, and North America. The Company has
established a significant presence in key emerging economies including Bangladesh, Egypt, Ethiopia, India, Kenya, the
Kingdom of Saudi Arabia, Morocco, Nicaragua, Nigeria, South Africa, Turkey, the United Arab Emirates, the United States of
America, and Tanzania.
Page 22

[page 24]
Fabtech
Technologies Limited
Annual Report 2025-26 | fabtechnologies.com Life Engineering
The Company continues to leverage its extensive technical expertise and robust infrastructure to deliver comprehensive
solutions for the establishment of aseptic manufacturing facilities, covering the entire value chain from conceptual design
to regulatory certification.
The Company offers end-to-end services for greenfield projects, including disease identification, project planning, design,
engineering, procurement, quality assurance, logistics management, as well as installation and commissioning. These
services are delivered to a diverse customer base across multiple geographies, with a particular focus on emerging
markets.
As an integrated turnkey engineering solutions provider, the Company plays a critical role in ensuring optimal utilization
of resources by delivering customized solutions tailored to the specific requirements of each project. By managing all
aspects of project execution from conception to completion, the Company ensures seamless coordination across various
stages, thereby enhancing efficiency and increasing the likelihood of successful project implementation.
a) Standalone Financial Statements:
During the year under review, the Company recorded revenue from operations on a standalone basis for FY 2025-26 at
Rs. 26,804.55 lakhs, registering a growth of 13.38% as compared to Rs. 23,642.35 lakhs in FY 2024-25. The profit for the year
stood at Rs. 2,425.30 lakhs as against Rs. 3,837.57 lakhs reported in FY 2024-25.
b) Consolidated Financial Statements:
During the year under review, the Company recorded revenue from operations on a consolidated basis for FY 2025—26 at
Rs. 41,077.18 lakhs, registering a growth of 25.75% as compared to Rs. 32,666.85 lakhs in FY 2024-25. The profit for the year
stood at Rs. 3,835.80 lakhs as against Rs. 4,645.29 lakhs reported in FY 2024-25.

CONSERVATION OF ENERGY, TECHNOLOGY ABSORPTION, FOREIGN EXCHANGE EARNINGS AND
OUTGO:
A. CONSERVATION OF ENERGY:
(i) Steps taken or impact on conservation of energy — The Operations of the Company are not energy intensive.
However, Company continues to implement prudent practices for saving electricity and other energy resources
in day-to-day activities.

Annexure C
Management Discussion and Analysis (MD&A)
This Management Discussion and Analysis (MD&A) has been prepared based on the audited financial
statements of Fabtech Technologies Limited for the financial year ended March 31, 2026.
(a) Industry Structure and Developments:
Fabtech Technologies Limited operates in the pharmaceutical engineering, cleanroom infrastructure, and
turnkey project solutions industry. The Company provides pharmaceutical machinery, engineering services,
cleanroom systems, process utilities, and turnkey project execution solutions for pharmaceutical and allied
industries.
The global pharmaceutical industry continues to witness strong long-term growth driven by increasing
healthcare expenditure, ageing population, rising demand for generic drugs, biologics, vaccines, and
specialty medicines. Industry estimates suggest that the global pharmaceutical market is expected to
exceed USD 1.9 trillion by 2027. Increasing investments in pharmaceutical manufacturing capacities,
biotechnology facilities, and regulated production environments continue to drive demand for
pharmaceutical engineering and turnkey solutions.
The global cleanroom technologies market is also witnessing robust growth due to increasing demand from
pharmaceutical, biotechnology, and healthcare manufacturing industries. Rising investments in sterile
manufacturing facilities, vaccine production plants, and compliance-driven infrastructure are creating
substantial demand for turnkey engineering and validation solutions.
India continues to remain one of the world's leading generic medicine manufacturing hubs and exports
pharmaceutical products to over 200 countries. Government initiatives such as Production Linked Incentive
(PLI) schemes, healthcare infrastructure investments, and focus on domestic pharmaceutical
manufacturing are expected to support future demand for engineering and turnkey project services.
The Company has strengthened its international presence across India, I-JAE, Saudi Arabia, Kenya, Algeria,
Egypt, and other overseas markets. During FY 2025-26, the Company expanded its footprint through
incorporation of Fabtech Lifecare Company in Saudi Arabia. Further, the Company successfully completed
its Initial Public Offering (IPO) and listed its shares on NSE and BSE in October 2025, improving financial
strength, visibility, and access to growth capital.
(b) Opportunities and Threats:
The pharmaceutical infrastructure and engineering industry offers substantial long-term opportunities
supported by increasing investments in regulated manufacturing infrastructure globally.
Key opportunities include:
Expansion of pharmaceutical manufacturing capacities globally.
Rising investments in cleanroom and sterile manufacturing infrastructure.
Growing outsourcing of turnkey engineering and project management solutions.
Increasing opportunities in Middle East, Africa, and emerging pharmaceutical markets.
Availability of IPO proceeds for working capital support and strategic expansion.
Potential inorganic growth opportunities through acquisitions and partnerships.
Page 42

[page 44]
Fabtech
Technologies Limited
Annual Report 2025-26 | fabtechnologies.com Life Engineering
Key threats and challenges include:
Foreign exchange volatility due to international operations.
Delays in project execution arising from supply chain disruptions.
Dependence on customer approvals and milestone-based billing.
Intense competition from domestic and international engineering companies.
• Geopolitical and regulatory risks in overseas territories.
Inflationary pressure on raw materials and logistics.
Risk Mitigation Measures:
The Company follows geographical diversification to reduce dependence on any single market.
Strong project monitoring systems and milestone tracking mechanisms are implemented to reduce
execution delays.
The Company maintains disciplined working capital management practices and regular customer
follow-ups for collections.
Long-standing customer relationships and diversified project portfolio reduce concentration risk.
Foreign exchange exposures are monitored continuously and natural hedging mechanisms are
adopted wherever feasible.
• Management regularly reviews procurement planning and vendor relationships to mitigate supply
chain disruptions.
Strong internal controls and periodic management reviews help ensure operational efficiency and
financial discipline.
(c) Segment-wise or Product-wise Performance:
The Company primarily derives its revenue from pharmaceutical turnkey project solutions and engineering
services provided across various geographical markets.
Consolidated revenue from operations for FY2025-26 increased to Rs. 41,077.18 lakhs as compared to Rs.
32,666.85 lakhs in FY2024-25, representing growth of approximately 25.8%.
The increase in revenue was primarily driven by strong overseas execution and expansion of international
business operations.
Geographical segment performance during FY2025-26 was as follows:
Rest of the World segment contributed Rs. 14,366.08 lakhs.
UAE operations contributed Rs. 12,666.47 lakhs.
Saudi Arabia contributed Rs. 8,272.36 lakhs.
India operations contributed Rs. 4,533.07 lakhs.
Kenya contributed Rs. 4,342.16 lakhs.
Algeria contributed Rs. 614.63 lakhs.
Egypt contributed Rs. 649.91 lakhs.
Trade receivables increased from Rs. 15,265.94 lakhs in FY2024-25 to Rs. 20,433.51 lakhs in FY2025-26, mainly
due to growth in project execution and increased international operations.
The Company continues to maintain a diversified international project portfolio, reducing overdependence
on any single market.

(d) Outlook:
The management outlook for the Company remains positive due to favorable growth trends in the
pharmaceutical manufacturing and engineering sectors globally.
The Company expects future growth to be supported by:
Strong order pipeline from international markets.
Increasing pharmaceutical infrastructure investments.
Expansion in Middle East and African markets.
Enhanced financial flexibility post IPO.
Growing demand for turnkey project execution capabilities.
Potential inorganic growth opportunities and strategic acquisitions.
The Company intends to utilize IPO proceeds towards:
• Working capital requirements.
Inorganic growth initiatives.
• General corporate purposes.
Management remains focused on improving execution efficiency, strengthening international operations,
and enhancing profitability through operational scale and disciplined financial management.
(e) Risks and Concerns
The Company remains exposed to operational, financial, regulatory, and external business risks associated
with domestic and international project execution.
Major risks and concerns include:
Foreign exchange fluctuation risk.
Delays in execution of projects.
Rising trade receivables and collection risk.
Dependence on customer approvals and milestone payments.
Geopolitical instability in overseas markets.
Inflationary pressure on raw materials and freight costs.
Working capital intensive business operations.
Global economic slowdown affecting pharmaceutical capital expenditure.
Risk Mitigation Framework:
The Company continuously monitors foreign exchange exposure and adopts prudent treasury and
hedging practices.
Project execution timelines are monitored through structured review systems and periodic
management oversight.
Customer credit evaluation and regular receivable monitoring are undertaken to improve collections
and reduce bad debt risk.
Diversified international operations reduce dependency on any single geography or customer
segment.
Vendor diversification and procurement planning help mitigate supply chain and pricing risks.
Strong internal financial controls and compliance systems reduce operational and regulatory risks.
• Management focuses on maintaining adequate liquidity buffers and strengthening cash flow
management practices.
• Continuous monitoring of geopolitical and regulatory developments enables proactive decision-
making and contingency planning.

expansion and business growth.
Other expenses increased due to higher business scale and operational activities.
Total assets increased significantly from Rs. 42,655.80 lakhs to Rs. 65,370.29 lakhs.
Equity increased substantially following IPO proceeds and retained earnings.
Cash and cash equivalents increased to Rs. 8,688.16 lakhs compared to Rs. 905.56 lakhs in the previous
year.
The Company generated strong financing cash flows through IPO proceeds which strengthened liquidity
and supported future expansion plans. However, operating cash flows were impacted by increased trade
receivables and working capital requirements associated with business growth.

ANNEXURE D
CORPORATE GOVERNANCE REPORT
The report on Corporate Governance is prepared for the financial year ended March 31, 2026 pursuant to
Regulation 34(3) read with Part C of Schedule V of the Securities and Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations
and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 ("SEBI Listing Regulations"), the report containing details of
Corporate Governance of Fabtech Technologies Limited ('the Company'/ 'Fabtech') is as follows:
1. COMPANY'S PHILOSOPHY ON CODE OF GOVERNANCE:
Fabtech Technologies Limited's ('the Company'/ 'Fabtech') philosophy firmly believes that sound corporate
governance practices are fundamental to enhancing stakeholder value and maintaining trust and transparency
in its operations. The Company's philosophy on corporate governance is rooted in the principles of accountability,
fairness, and transparency in all its dealings.
The Company is committed to achieving and maintaining the highest standards of corporate governance by
adhering to applicable laws, rules, and regulations, including those prescribed by the Securities and Exchange
Board of India ("SEBI") and the stock exchanges. The Company recognizes that good governance is a continuous
process and strives to continuously reviewing and to align its practices with evolving regulatory requirements and
global best practices.
The Company has established robust internal policies, codes, and procedures to guide its operations and
decision-making processes. The Company also places significant emphasis on compliance with the provisions of
the Companies Act, 2013 and the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015, and
ensures that systems are in place for adherence to these requirements.
The Company remains committed to continually improving its governance practices and upholding the highest
standards of ethics, transparency, and accountability to create sustainable value for all its stakeholders.
Governance Policies
The Company has also adopted various policies and codes in line with regulatory requirements and best
practices, including but not limited to code of conduct for directors and senior management, Code of Practices
and Procedures for Fair Disclosure of Unpublished Price Sensitive Information, Code of Conduct for Regulating,
Monitoring & Reporting of Trades and Prevention of Insider Trading and other policies. These policies are
periodically reviewed and updated to ensure their continued relevance and effectiveness, and are made available
on the Company's website at https://fabtechnologies.com/policies/for stakeholders' access.

The Board meets at regular intervals to discuss and decide on Company / business policies and strategies apart
from other regular business matters. The information, as required under Regulation 17(7) read with Schedule II Part
A of the SEBI Listing Regulations, is made available to the Board. All committee recommendations placed before

discussions held at the Committee meetings.

Chairman and circulates the same in advance to the Directors. However, in case of urgency, the agenda is
circulated along with shorter notice as per the provisions of the Secretarial Standard on Meetings of the Board of
Directors issued by the Institute of Company Secretaries of India.
The Board meets at least once in a quarter to inter-alia review the quarterly results, compliances and performance
of the Company. Additional meetings are held on need basis. The Company also provides facility to the Directors

Independent Directors
The Company recognizes the critical role played by Independent Directors in ensuring a high standard of
corporate governance. The Independent Directors bring an objective and independent view to the Board's
deliberations and decision-making process and provide strategic guidance, thereby contributing to the overall
efficiency of the Board.
In terms of the provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 and the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure
Requirements) Regulations, 2015 ('SEBI Listing Regulations'), the Company has an optimum combination of
Executive, Non-Executive and Independent Directors on its Board. The Independent Directors fulfil the conditions of
independence as specified under the aforesaid Act and SEBI Listing Regulations and are persons of integrity and
possess relevant expertise and experience.
The Company has received declarations from all Independent Directors confirming that they meet the criteria of
independence as prescribed under Section 149(6) of the Companies Act, 2013 and Regulation 16(I)(b) of the SEBI
Listing Regulations. In the opinion of the Board, the Independent Directors of the Company are independent of the
management. The Company had also issued formal appointment letters to all the Independent Directors at the
time of appointment in the manner provided under the Act. Terms and Conditions for appointment of Independent
Directors are available on website of the Company at https://fabtechnologies.com/wp-
content/ uploads/ 2024/09/ Terms-And-Conditions-Of-Appointment-Of-lndependent-Directors.pdf
The Independent Directors are not related to promoters or directors of the Company and do not have any
pecuniary relationship with the Company, other than sitting fees and reimbursement of expenses incurred for
participation in Board and Committee meetings, as permitted under applicable laws.
The Independent Directors actively participate in the meetings of the Board and its Committees and provide
valuable guidance in areas such as strategy, performance, risk management, internal controls, and governance
standards. They also ensure that the interests of minority shareholders are adequately safeguarded.

Directors and members of the management.

without the presence of Non-lndependent Directors and members of management. At the said meeting, the
Independent Directors, inter alia, reviewed the performance of Non-lndependent Directors and the Board as a
whole, assessed the quality, quantity and timeliness of flow of information between the Company management
and the Board, and evaluated the performance of the Chairperson of the Company, taking into account the views
of Executive and Non-Executive Directors.

3. COMMITTEES OF THE BOARD:
Audit Committee
The Audit Committee of the Board is constituted in accordance with the provisions of Regulation 18 of the SEBI
Listing Regulations read with Section 177 of the Companies Act, 2013. The Committee oversees the Company's
financial reporting process, ensures the adequacy of internal control systems, reviews audit findings, and monitors
compliance with legal and regulatory requirements.
Terms of Reference of Audit Committee
The scope and functions of the Audit Committee are in accordance with Section 177 of the Companies Act and
Regulation 18 of the SEBI Listing Regulations and its terms of reference are as disclosed below:
The Audit Committee shall be responsible for, among other things, as may be required by the stock exchange(s)
from time to time, the following:
A. Powers of Audit Committee
The Audit Committee shall have powers, including the following:
(1) to investigate any activity within its terms of reference;
(2) to seek information from any employee;
(3) to obtain outside legal or other professional advice;
(4) to secure attendance of outsiders with relevant expertise, if it considers necessary; and
(5) such other powers as may be prescribed under the Companies Act and SEBI Listing Regulations.

Role of Audit Committee
The role of the Audit Committee shall include the following:
oversight of financial reporting process and the disclosure of financial information relating to Fabtech
Technologies Limited (the "Company") to ensure that the financial statements are correct, sufficient and
credible;
(2) recommendation for appointment, re-appointment, replacement, remuneration and terms of appointment
of auditors of the Company and the fixation of the audit fee;
(3) approval of payment to statutory auditors for any other services rendered by the statutory auditors;
(4) formulation of a policy on related party transactions, which shall include materiality of related party
transactions;
(5) reviewing, at least on a quarterly basis, the details of related party transactions entered into by the Company
pursuant to each of the omnibus approvals given;
(6) examining and reviewing, with the management, the annual financial statements and auditor's report
thereon before submission to the Board for approval, with particular reference to:
a. Matters required to be included in the director's responsibility statement to be included in the Board's
report in terms of clause (c) of sub-section 3 of section 134 of the Companies Act, 2013;
b. Changes, if any, in accounting policies and practices and reasons for the same;
c. Major accounting entries involving estimates based on the exercise of judgment by management;
d. Significant adjustments made in the financial statements arising out of audit findings;
e. Compliance with listing and other legal requirements relating to financial statements;
f. Disclosure of any related party transactions; and
g. Modified opinion(s) in the draft audit report.
(7) reviewing, with the management, the quarterly, half-yearly and annual financial statements before
submission to the Board for approval;
(8) reviewing, with the management, the statement of uses / application of funds raised through an issue (public
issue, rights issue, preferential issue, etc.), the statement of funds utilised for purposes other than those stated
in the Issue document / prospectus / notice and the report submitted by the monitoring agency monitoring
the utilisation of proceeds of a public or rights issue, and making appropriate recommendations to the board
of directors of the Company (the "Board" or "Board of Directors") to take up steps in this matter;
(9) reviewing and monitoring the auditor's independence and performance, and effectiveness of audit process;
(10) approval of any subsequent modification of transactions of the Company with related parties and omnibus
approval for related party transactions proposed to be entered into by the Company, subject to the
conditions as may be prescribed;
Explanation: The term "related party transactions" shall have the same meaning as provided in Clause 2(zc)
of the SEBI Listing Regulations and/or the applicable Accounting Standards and/or the Companies Act, 2013.
scrutiny of inter-corporate loans and investments;

II. Nomination and Remuneration Committee
The Nomination and Remuneration Committee (NRC) is constituted in line with the provisions of the Companies
Act, 2013 and Regulation 19 of the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015. The
Committee is responsible for identifying and recommending individuals for appointment to the Board and senior
management, evaluating the performance of Directors. It also oversees the remuneration policy of the Company
to ensure that it is aligned with performance, industry standards, and the long-term interests of stakeholders.
Terms of Reference of Nomination and Remuneration Committee
The Nomination and Remuneration Committee shall be responsible for, among other things, the following, in
supersession of the previous terms of reference of the Nomination and Remuneration Committee:
(1) Formulation of the criteria for determining qualifications, positive attributes and independence of a director
and recommend to the board of directors of the Company (the "Board" or "Board of Directors") a policy
relating to the remuneration of the directors, key managerial personnel and other employees
("Remuneration Policy").
The Nomination and Remuneration Committee, while formulating the above policy, should ensure that:
the level and composition of remuneration be reasonable and sufficient to attract, retain and
motivate directors of the quality required to run our Company successfully
relationship of remuneration to performance is clear and meets appropriate performance
benchmarks; and
(iii) remuneration to directors, key managerial personnel and senior management involves a
balance between fixed and incentive pay reflecting short- and long-term performance
objectives appropriate to the working of the Company and its goals.
(2) For every appointment of an independent director, evaluating the balance of skills, knowledge and
experience on the Board and on the basis of such evaluation, preparing a description of the role and
capabilities required of an independent director. The person recommended to the Board for appointment as
an independent director shall have the capabilities identified in such description. For the purpose of
identifying suitable candidates, the Nomination and Remuneration Committee may: (a) use the services of
an external agencies, if required; (b) consider candidates from a wide range of backgrounds, having due
regard to diversity; and (c) consider the time commitments of the candidates.
(3) Formulation of criteria for evaluation of independent directors and the Board;
(4) Devising a policy on Board diversity;
(5) Identifying persons who are qualified to become directors and who may be appointed in senior
management in accordance with the criteria laid down, and recommend to the Board their appointment
and removal and carrying out evaluation of every director's performance (including independent director);
(6) Analysing, monitoring and reviewing various human resource and compensation matters;
Page 58

[page 60]
Fabtech
Technologies Limited
Annual Report 2025-26 | fabtechnologies.com Life Engineering
(7) Deciding whether to extend or continue the term of appointment of the independent director, on the basis of
the report of performance evaluation of independent directors;
(8) Determining the Company's policy on specific remuneration packages for executive directors including
pension rights and any compensation payment, and determining remuneration packages of such directors;
(9) Recommending to the board, all remuneration, in whatever form, payable to senior management and other
staff, as deemed necessary;
(IO) Carrying out any other functions required to be carried out by the Nomination and Remuneration Committee

(12) Perform such functions as are required to be performed by the compensation committee under the

if applicable;
(a) To administer the employee stock option scheme/plan approved by the Board and shareholders of the
Company in accordance with the terms of such scheme/plan ("ESOP Scheme") including the following:
determining the eligibility of employees to participate under the ESOP Scheme;
determining the quantum of option to be granted under the ESOP Scheme per employee and in
aggregate;
iii. date of grant;
iv. determining the exercise price of the option under the ESOP Scheme;
v. the conditions under which option may vest in employee and may lapse in case of termination of
employment for misconduct;
vi. the exercise period within which the employee should exercise the option and that option would
lapse on failure to exercise the option within the exercise period;
vii. the specified time period within which the employee shall exercise the vested option in the event
of termination or resignation of an employee;
viii. the right of an employee to exercise all the options vested in him at one time or at various points
of time within the exercise period;
ix. re-pricing of the options which are not exercised, whether or not they have been vested if stock
option rendered unattractive due to fall in the market price of the equity shares;
x. the grant, vest and exercise of option in case of employees who are on long leave;
xi. allow exercise of unvested options on such terms and conditions as it may deem fit;
xii. the procedure for cashless exercise of options;
xiii. forfeiture/ cancellation of options granted;
xiv. formulating and implementing the procedure for making a fair and reasonable adjustment to the
number of options and to the exercise price in case of corporate actions such as rights issues,
bonus issues, merger, sale of division and others. In this regard following shall be taken into
consideration:
the number and the price of stock option shall be adjusted in a manner such that total value
of the option to the employee remains the same after the corporate action;
for this purpose, follow global best practices in this area including the procedures followed by
the derivative markets in India and abroad may be considered; and
the vesting period and the life of the option shall be left unaltered as far as possible to protect
the rights of the employee who is granted such option.

(15) Perform such other activities as may be delegated by the Board or specified/ provided under the Companies
Act, 2013 to the extent notified and effective, as amended or by the Securities and Exchange Board of India
(Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015, as amended or by any other applicable
law or regulatory authority.
(16) To consider any other matters as may be requested by the Board; and
(17) To make available its terms of reference and review annually those terms of reference and its own
effectiveness and recommend any necessary changes to the Board.
The committee is authorised by the Board to:
(a) investigate any activity within its terms of reference;
(b) seek any information from any employee of the Company or any associate or subsidiary, joint venture
Company in order to perform its duties and all employees are directed by the Board to co-operate with
any request made by the Committee; and
(c) call any director or other employee to be present at a meeting of the Committee as and when required.
If the Committee considers it necessary so to do it is authorised to obtain appropriate external advice
including but not limited to legal and professional advice to assist it in the performance of its duties and
to secure the services of outsiders with relevant experience and expertise and to invite those persons to
attend at meetings of the Committee. The cost of obtaining any advice or services shall be paid by the
Company within the limits as authorised by the Board.

Evaluation of Board, Individual Directors, Committees of Board and Criteria for Evaluation
The Companies Act, 2013 states that a formal annual evaluation needs to be made by the Board of its own
performance and that of its committees and individual Directors. Schedule IV to the Companies Act, 2013 states
that the performance evaluation of Independent Directors shall be done by the entire Board of Directors, excluding
the director being evaluated.
The annual performance evaluation of the Board and its Committees was carried out through a structured
evaluation process covering various aspects of the Board's functioning such as composition of the Board &
Committees, experience & competencies, performance etc. This also included evaluation of performance of the
individual Directors and Independent Directors who were evaluated on parameters such as Attendance, Decision-
Making, Contribution at the meetings, and otherwise, Independent Judgment, Problem Solving, etc.
Both, the Nomination and Remuneration Committee and the Board, were satisfied with the evaluation process,
which reflected the overall engagement of the Board and its Committees with the Company.
Further, the Independent Directors in a separate meeting of Independent Directors held on January 15, 2026
evaluated the performance of Non-lndependent Directors, performance of the Board as a whole and performance
of the Chairperson.
The evaluation of Directors/ Chairperson was carried out by the entire Board, excluding the Director being
evaluated.
Ill. Stakeholders' Relationship Committee
The Stakeholders' Relationship Committee is constituted in accordance with the provisions of the Companies Act,
2013 and Regulation 20 of the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015. The
Committee focuses on the redressal of grievances of shareholders and other security holders. It also oversees
measures for improving investor services and strengthening stakeholder relationships.
Page 61

[page 63]
Fabtech
Technologies Limited
Annual Report 2025-26 | fabtechnologies.com Life Engineering
Terms of Reference of Stakeholders' Relationship Committee
The Stakeholders' Relationship Committee shall be responsible for, among other things, as may be required by
the under applicable law, the following:
(1) Resolving the grievances of the security holders of the listed entity including complaints related to transfer
of shares or debentures, including non-receipt of share or debenture certificates and review of cases for
refusal of transfer/transmission of shares and debentures, non-receipt of annual report or balance sheet,
non-receipt of declared dividends, issue of new/duplicate certificates, general meetings etc. and assisting
with quarterly reporting of such complaints;
(2) Review of measures taken for effective exercise of voting rights by shareholders;
(3) Investigating complaints relating to allotment of shares, approval of transfer or transmission of shares,
debentures or any other securities
(4) Giving effect to all transfer/transmission of shares and debentures, dematerialisation of shares and re-
materialisation of shares, split and issue of duplicate/consolidated share certificates, compliance with all the
requirements related to shares, debentures and other securities from time to time
(5) Review of adherence to the service standards adopted by the listed entity in respect of various services being
rendered by the registrar and share transfer agent of the Company and to recommend measures for overall
improvement in the quality of investor services;
(6) Review of the various measures and initiatives taken by the listed entity for reducing the quantum of
unclaimed dividends and ensuring timely receipt of dividend warrants/annual reports/statutory notices by
the shareholders of the company;
(7) To approve allotment of shares, debentures or any other securities as per the authority conferred / to be
conferred to the Committee by the Board of Directors from time to time;
(8) To approve requests for transfer, transposition, deletion, consolidation, sub-division, change of name,
dematerialization, rematerialisation etc. of shares, debentures and other securities;
(9) To monitor and expedite the status and process of dematerialization and rematerialisation of shares,
debentures and other securities of the Company;
(10) Carrying out such other functions as may be specified by the Board from time to time or specified/provided
under the Companies Act or SEBI Listing Regulations, or by any other regulatory authority; and
(11) Such terms of reference as may be prescribed under the Companies Act and SEBI Listing Regulations.

IV. Corporate Social Responsibility Committee
The Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) Committee is constituted in accordance with the provisions of the
Companies Act, 2013. The Committee is entrusted with the responsibility of formulating and recommending the
CSR policy, identifying and monitoring CSR activities, and ensuring that the Company undertakes initiatives in
areas specified under Schedule VII of the Act.
Terms of Reference of Corporate Social Responsibility Committee
The scope and functions of the CSR Committee are in accordance with Section 135 of the Companies Act, 2013 and
other applicable law and its terms of reference include the following:
Page 63

[page 65]
Fabtech
Technologies Limited
Annual Report 2025-26 | fabtechnologies.com Life Engineering
(a) formulate and recommend to the Board, a "Corporate Social Responsibility Policy" which shall indicate the
activities to be undertaken by the Company as specified in Schedule VII of the Companies Act, 2013 and
the rules made thereunder, as amended, monitor the implementation of the same from time to time, and
make any revisions therein as and when decided by the Board;
(b) identify corporate social responsibility policy partners and corporate social responsibility policy
programmes;
(c) review and recommend the amount of expenditure to be incurred on the activities referred to in clause (a)
and the distribution of the same to various corporate social responsibility programs undertaken by the
Company;
(d) delegate responsibilities to the corporate social responsibility team and supervise proper execution of all
delegated responsibilities;
review and monitor the implementation of corporate social responsibility programmes and issuing
necessary directions as required for proper implementation and timely completion of corporate social
responsibility programmes;
any other matter as the Corporate Social Responsibility Committee may deem appropriate after approval
of the Board or as may be directed by the Board, from time to time;
(g) The Corporate Social Responsibility Committee shall formulate and recommend to the Board, an annual
action plan in pursuance of its corporate social responsibility policy, which shall include the following:
(i) the list of corporate social responsibility projects or programmes that are approved to be undertaken
in areas or subjects specified in Schedule VII of the Companies Act;
(ii) the manner of execution of such projects or programmes as specified in the rules notified under the
Companies Act;
(iii) the modalities of utilisation of funds and implementation schedules for the projects or programmes;
(iv) monitoring and reporting mechanism for the projects or programmes; and
(v) details of need and impact assessment, if any, for the projects undertaken by the Company;
exercise such other powers as may be conferred upon the Corporate Social Responsibility Committee in
terms of the provisions of Section 135 of the Companies Act.

V. Risk Management Committee
Pursuant to Regulation 21 of the Securities and Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure
Requirements) Regulations, 2015, read with other applicable provisions, the Company is not mandatorily required
to constitute a Risk Management Committee based on the applicability criteria prescribed thereunder.
Accordingly, the Board of Directors, at its meeting held on February 02, 2026, approved the dissolution of the Risk
Management Committee with immediate effect. The oversight of risk management functions of the Company
continues to be undertaken by the Audit Committee and/or the Board of Directors, as may be applicable.
Terms of Reference of Risk Management Committee
The terms of reference of the Risk Management Committee are as follows:
To formulate a detailed risk management policy which shall include:
framework for identification of internal and external risks specifically faced by the Company, in
particular including financial, operational, sectoral, sustainability (particularly, Environmental, Social
and Governance (ESG) related risks), information, cyber security risks or any other risk as may be
determined;
Measures for risk mitigation including systems and processes for internal control of identified risks;
and
Business continuity plan.
To ensure that appropriate methodology, processes and systems are in place to monitor and evaluate risks
associated with the business of the Company;
(iii) To monitor and oversee implementation of the risk management policy, including evaluating the adequacy
of risk management systems;
(iv) To periodically review the risk management policy, at least once in two years, including by considering the
changing industry dynamics and evolving complexity;
(v) To keep the board of directors informed about the nature and content of its discussions, recommendations
and actions to be taken;
(vi) The appointment, removal and terms of remuneration of the Chief Risk Officer shall be subject to review by
the Risk Management Committee.
Page 65

[page 67]
Fabtech
Technologies Limited
Annual Report 2025-26 | fabtechnologies.com Life Engineering
(vii) To seek information from any employee, obtain outside legal or other professional advice and secure
attendance of outsiders with relevant expertise, if it considers necessary.
(viii) Laying down risk assessment and minimization procedures and the procedures to inform Board of the
same;
Framing, implementing, reviewing and monitoring the risk management plan for the Company and such
other functions, including cyber security, as may be delegated by the Board; and
Performing such other activities as may be delegated by the Board and/or are statutorily prescribed under
any law to be attended to by the Risk Management Committee.

5. REMUNERATION OF DIRECTORS
Nomination and Remuneration Policy
The Company has a well-defined Nomination and Remuneration Policy pursuant to provisions of Section 178 of the
Companies Act and Para A of Part D of Schedule II to the Listing Regulations, which lays down the criteria for
determining, inter-alia, qualifications, positive attributes and independence of a Director and matters relating to
the remuneration, appointment, removal and evaluation of performance of the Directors.
The detailed policy formulated by Nomination and Remuneration Committee is annexed to the Board's Report and
can be accessed on the website of the Company at
https://fabtechnologies.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/NRC- Policy-I .pdf
i. Pecuniary Relationship or Transactions of Non-Executive Directors vis-d-vis the listed entity:
There is no pecuniary or business relationship between the Non-Executive/lndependent Directors and the
Company, except for the sitting fees for attending meetings of the Board/committees thereof and the payment
of commission on the profits of the company, if approved by the Board of Directors and as allowed under the
Companies Act, 2013.
ii. Criteria of making Payment to Non-Executive/ Independent Directors:
The criteria of making payment to Non-Executive/lndependent Directors forms part of the Nomination and
Remuneration policy which is available on the website of the Company and can be accessed at the weblink:
https://fabtechnologies.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/NRC-Policy-1.pdf. The policy includes the following
details:
The Non- Executive / Independent Directors may receive sitting fees and such other remuneration as
permissible under the provisions of Act. The amount of sitting fees shall be such as may be recommended
by the Nomination and Remuneration Committee and approved by the Board of Directors.
All remuneration, fees, commission (except sitting fees) payable to Non-Executive Directors shall be subject
to members' approval.
The Independent Directors shall not be eligible to get stock options and also shall not be eligible to participate
in any share-based payment schemes of the Company.
The remuneration to the Non-executive Directors (including Independent Directors) may be paid within the
monetary limit approved by shareholders, subject to the limit not exceeding 1% of the profits of the Company
computed as per the applicable provisions of the Act.

8. MEANS OF COMMUNICATION
I. Quarterly Results and Newspaper Advertisement
Quarterly Results of the Company are regularly submitted to the National Stock Exchange of India Limited (NSE)
and BSE Limited (BSE) within forty-five days from the end of quarter and the annual audited financial statements
are announced within sixty days from end of financial year as required under the SEBI Listing Regulations. The same
are also uploaded on the website of the Company under Investors Tab and can be accessed at
The results are usually published in Financial Express (English newspaper having country-wide circulation) and in
Loksatta (Marathi newspaper) where the registered office of the Company is situated. These newspaper
advertisements are displayed on the website of the Company
https://fabtechnologies.com/intimation-to-stock-exchange/
II. Any website, where displayed
The Company's website has a separate Investors Tab wherein all the information / documents related to Investors
such as Annual Reports, Board of Directors, Committees of the Board, Announcements to Stock Exchange, policies,
etc are available. All the details required in terms of SEBI Listing Regulations is also provided on the Company's
website and the same is updated regularly. The investors can access the same at https://fabtechnologies.com/
under Investors Tab.

Vigil Mechanism and Whistle-Blower Policy and affirmation that no personnel has been denied access to the
Chairperson of the Audit Committee
The Company has established a Vigil Mechanism and adopted a Whistle-Blower Policy in accordance with the
provisions of Section 177 of the Companies Act, 2013 and the SEBI Listing Regulations. The Policy provides a formal
mechanism for directors and employees to report instances of any fraud/ misconduct/ violation of Code or
employment policies without any fear of reprisal or victimization for whistle-blowing in good faith, without
necessarily informing their supervisors.
The mechanism ensures adequate safeguards against victimisation of persons who avail of the mechanism and
provides for direct access to the Chairperson of the Audit Committee in appropriate cases. The Policy is available
on the website of the Company and can be accessed using the link: https://fabtechnologies.com/policies/
The Company affirms that no personnel has been denied access to the Chairperson of the Audit Committee during
the financial year.

stewardship, creating a positive and lasting impact on communities and the environment. The CSR Policy focuses
on areas such as education, healthcare and environmental sustainability, as specified under Schedule VII of the
Act. CSR activities are undertaken either directly by the Company or through eligible registered implementing
agencies.

Independent Auditor's Report
To the Members of Fabtech Technologies Limited
(Formerly Fabtech Technologies Private Limited)
Report on the Audit of the Standalone Financial Statements
Opinion
We have audited the accompanying Standalone Financial Statements of Fabtech Technologies Limited (Formerly known as
Fabtech Technologies Private Limited) (the "Company"), which comprise the Standalone Balance Sheet as at March 31, 2026,
and the Standalone Statement of Profit and Loss (including Other Comprehensive Income), Standalone Statement of Cash Flows
and the Standalone Statement of Changes in Equity for the year ended on that date and notes to the standalone financial
statements, including the summary of the material accounting policies and other explanatory information (hereinafter referred
to as the "Standalone Financial Statements").
In our opinion and to the best of our information and according to the explanations given to us, the aforesaid standalone
financial statements give the information required by the Companies Act, 2013 ("the Act") in the manner so required and give a
true and fair view in conformity with the Indian Accounting Standards prescribed under section 133 of the Act read with
the Companies (Indian Accounting Standards) Rules, 2015, as amended, ("Ind AS") and other accounting principles generally
accepted in India, of the state of affairs of the Company as at March 31, 2026, its profit including other comprehensive Income, its
cash flows and the changes in equity for the year ended on that date.
Basis for opinion
We conducted our audit of the Standalone Financial Statements in accordance with the Standards on Auditing ("SA"s) specified
under section 143(10) of the Act. Our responsibilities under those Standards are further described in the Auditor's Responsibilities
for the Audit of the Standalone Financial Statements section of our report. We are independent of the Company in accordance
with the Code of Ethics issued by the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India ("ICAI") together with the ethical requirements
that are relevant to our audit of the Standalone Financial Statements under the provisions of the Act and the Rules made
thereunder, and we have fulfilled our other ethical responsibilities in accordance with these requirements and the ICAl's Code of
Ethics. We believe that the audit evidence obtained by us is sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for our audit opinion on
the Standalone Financial Statements.
Key Audit Matters
Key audit matters are those matters that, in our professional judgment, were of most significance in our audit of the Standalone
Financial Statements of the current period. These matters were addressed in the context of our audit of the Standalone Financial
Statements as a whole and in forming our opinion thereon, and we do not provide a separate opinion on these matters.
We have determined the matters described below to be the key audit matters to be communicated in our report:
Key Audit Matters Response to Key Audit Matters & Conclusion
Recoverability and valuation of allowance for impairment of Our audit procedures included but were not limited to the

Trade receivables, including retention money with customers Obtained an understanding of the process adopted
forms a significant part of the Standalone Financial by the Company in estimating expected credit loss
Statements. Customer contracts typically involve time including the key inputs and assumptions. Since
consuming and complex conditions around closure of assumptions and parameters are based on historical
contracts, including technical acceptances. This generally data and available forward looking information with
leads to longer and significant time for realization of respect to Trade Receivables, we assessed whether
receivables. As described in Note 13 to the Standalone historical experience and other information was

Information Other than the Standalone Financial Statements and Auditor's Report Thereon
The Company's Management and Board of Directors are responsible for the other information. The other information comprises
the information included in the Company's Annual Report, but does not include the Consolidated Financial Statements,
Standalone Financial Statements and our auditor's report thereon.
Our opinion on the Standalone Financial Statements does not cover the other information and we do not express any form of
assurance conclusion thereon.
In connection with our audit of the Standalone Financial Statements, our responsibility is to read the other information and, in
doing so, consider whether the other information is materially inconsistent with the Standalone Financial Statements or our
knowledge obtained during the course of our audit or otherwise appears to be materially misstated.
If based on the work we have performed, we conclude that there is a material misstatement of this other information, we are
required to report that fact. We have nothing to report in this regard.
Responsibilities of Management and Those Charged with Governance for the Standalone Financial Statements
The Company's Management and Board of Directors are responsible for the matters stated in section 134(5) of the Act with
respect to the preparation and presentation of these Standalone Financial Statements that give a true and fair view of the
financial position, financial performance including other comprehensive income, cash flows and changes in equity of the
Company in accordance with the Ind AS specified under section 133 of the Act and other accounting principles generally
accepted in India. This responsibility also includes maintenance of adequate accounting records in accordance with the
provisions of the Act for safeguarding of the assets of the Company and for preventing and detecting frauds and other
irregularities; selection and application of appropriate accounting policies; making judgments and estimates that are
reasonable and prudent; and design, implementation and maintenance of adequate internal financial controls, that were
operating effectively for ensuring the accuracy and completeness of the accounting records, relevant to the preparation and
presentation of the standalone financial statements that give a true and fair view and are free from material misstatement,
whether due to fraud or error.
In preparing the Standalone Financial Statements, management and the Board of Directors are responsible for assessing the
Company's ability to continue as a going concern, disclosing, as applicable, matters related to going concern and using the
going concern basis of accounting unless the Board of Directors either intend to liquidate the Company or to cease operations,
or has no realistic alternative but to do so.
The Company's Board of Directors are also responsible for overseeing the Company's financial reporting process.
Auditor's Responsibility for the Audit of the Standalone Financial Statements
Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the Standalone Financial Statements as a whole are free from
material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error, and to issue an auditor's report that includes our opinion. Reasonable
assurance is a high level of assurance but is not a guarantee that an audit conducted in accordance with SAS will always detect
a material misstatement when it exists. Misstatements can arise from fraud or error and are considered material if, individually
Page 86

[page 88]
Fabtech
Technologies Limited
Annual Report 2025-26 | fabtechnologies.com Life Engineering
or in the aggregate, they could reasonably be expected to influence the economic decisions of users taken on the basis of these
Standalone Financial Statements.
As part of an audit in accordance with SAs, we exercise professional judgment and maintain professional skepticism throughout the
audit. We also:
Identify and assess the risks of material misstatement of the Standalone Financial Statements, whether due to fraud or
error, design and perform audit procedures responsive to those risks, and obtain audit evidence that is sufficient and
appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion. The risk of not detecting a material misstatement resulting from fraud is
higher than for one resulting from error, as fraud may involve collusion, forgery, intentional omissions, misrepresentations,
or the override of internal controls.
Obtain an understanding of internal financial controls relevant to the audit in order to design audit procedures that are
appropriate in the circumstances. Under section 143(3)(i) of the Act, we are also responsible for expressing our opinion on
whether the Company has adequate internal financial controls system in place and the operating effectiveness of such
controls.
Evaluate the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of accounting estimates and related
disclosures made by the management.
Conclude on the appropriateness of management's use of the going concern basis of accounting and, based on the audit
evidence obtained, whether a material uncertainty exists related to events or conditions that may cast significant doubt on
the Company's ability to continue as a going concern. If we conclude that a material uncertainty exists, we are required to
draw attention in our auditor's report to the related disclosures in the Standalone Financial Statements or, if such disclosures
are inadequate, to modify our opinion. Our conclusions are based on the audit evidence obtained up to the date of our
auditor's report. However, future events or conditions may cause the Company to cease to continue as a going concern.
Evaluate the overall presentation, structure, and content of the Standalone Financial Statements, including the disclosures,
and whether the Standalone Financial Statements represent the underlying transactions and events in a manner that
achieves fair presentation.
Materiality is the magnitude of misstatements in the Standalone Financial Statements that, individually or in aggregate, makes it
probable that the economic decisions of a reasonably knowledgeable user of the standalone financial statements may be
influenced. We consider quantitative materiality and qualitative factors (i) in planning the scope of our audit work and in evaluating
the results of our work; and (ii) to evaluate the effect of any identified misstatements in the Standalone Financial Statements.
We communicate with those charged with governance regarding, among other matters, the planned scope and timing of the audit
and significant audit findings, including any significant deficiencies in internal control that we identify during our audit.
We also provide those charged with governance with a statement that we have complied with relevant ethical requirements
regarding independence, and to communicate with them all relationships and other matters that may reasonably be thought to
bear on our independence, and where applicable, related safeguards.
From the matters communicated with those charged with governance, we determine those matters that were of most significance
in the audit of the Standalone Financial Statements of the current period and are therefore the key audit matters. We describe these
matters in our auditor's report unless law or regulation precludes public disclosure about the matter or when, in extremely rare
circumstances, we determine that a matter should not be communicated in our report because the adverse consequences of doing
so would reasonably be expected to outweigh the benefits of public interest of such communication.
